<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> The compact set of rank-one orthogonal projectors on ℝ<sup>4</sup>, with the Frobenius metric, cannot be covered by ten sets of strictly smaller diameter. It therefore gives a counterexample to Borsuk's conjecture in dimension nine.

- **C1** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): The compact set $$X=\{uu^{\mathsf T}:u\in\mathbb R^4,\ \\|u\\|=1\} \subset\{A\in\operatorname{Sym}_4(\mathbb R):\operatorname{tr}A=1\},$$ with the Frobenius metric, has diameter $\sqrt2$ and cannot be covered by ten subsets of diameter strictly less than $\sqrt2$.
  - Explanation: States that the compact set of rank-one orthogonal projectors in four dimensions with the Frobenius metric has diameter equal to the square root of two and cannot be covered by ten sets of strictly smaller diameter, yielding a counterexample to the partition conjecture of Borsuk in dimension nine.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-projector-geometry)): The map $x\mapsto P_x$ identifies $\mathbb P(V)$ homeomorphically with the compact set $X_k$ in the trace-one affine subspace of $\operatorname{Sym}(V)$. This affine subspace is Euclidean of dimension $N_k-1$. If $u,v$ are unit representatives of $x,y$, then $$\\|P_x-P_y\\|_F^2=2-2\langle u,v\rangle^2. \label{eq:projector-distance}$$ Consequently $\operatorname{diam}(X_k)=\sqrt2$, and two projectors are at this distance exactly when their lines are orthogonal.
  - Explanation: Establishes that mapping each projective line to its rank-one orthogonal projector embeds real projective space homeomorphically into the trace-one affine subspace of symmetric operators, where two projectors attain the maximum Frobenius distance precisely when their lines are orthogonal.
- **C3** (construction, [source](#stmt-auto-remark-1)): An explicit isometry from the trace-one symmetric $4\times4$ matrices to $\mathbb R^9$ is $$\begin{split} A\longmapsto\bigg(&\frac{a_{11}-a_{22}}{\sqrt2},\; \frac{a_{11}+a_{22}-2a_{33}}{\sqrt6},\; \frac{a_{11}+a_{22}+a_{33}-3a_{44}}{\sqrt{12}},\\ &\sqrt2a_{12},\sqrt2a_{13},\sqrt2a_{14}, \sqrt2a_{23},\sqrt2a_{24},\sqrt2a_{34}\bigg). \end{split} \label{eq:euclidean-coordinates}$$ The first three coordinates use an orthonormal basis of the trace-zero diagonal matrices. The factors $\sqrt2$ in the remaining six coordinates account for the two equal off-diagonal entries in the Frobenius norm. Thus the nine-dimensional witness will be $X_4$ with exactly its stated metric.
  - Explanation: Gives an explicit nine-coordinate Euclidean isometry from the trace-one affine hyperplane of four-by-four real symmetric matrices with the Frobenius metric onto nine-dimensional Euclidean space.
- **C4** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cover-to-map)): If $X_k$ is covered by at most $m$ subsets, each of diameter strictly less than $\sqrt2$, then there is an admissible map $f:\mathbb P(V)\to\mathbb R^m$.
  - Explanation: Proves that covering the rank-one projector set by at most a given number of subsets of diameter strictly less than the square root of two yields a smooth admissible map from projective space into Euclidean space via open neighborhood thickening and a smooth partition of unity.
- **C5** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-positive-extension)): The map $E_V$ is continuous on the full positive semidefinite cone, including its singular boundary. For $t\geq0$ it satisfies $$E_V(tQ)=tE_V(Q),\qquad E_V(Q)_i\geq0,\qquad \sum_{i=1}^m E_V(Q)_i=\operatorname{tr}Q.$$ Its positive coordinates are exactly the labels used on the range of $Q$: $$\{i:E_V(Q)_i>0\} =\bigcup_{x\in\mathbb P(\operatorname{ran}Q)}S(x), \label{eq:extension-support}$$ where the right side is empty for $Q=0$. Moreover:* 1. *$E_V(P_x)=f(x)$ for every line $x$;* 2. *if $Q$ is supported on a nonzero subspace $W\subseteq V$, then $E_V(Q)=E_W(Q\|_W)$, where the latter construction uses $f\|_{\mathbb P(W)}$ and the factor $\dim W$;* 3. *this last expression is smooth when $W$ varies smoothly with fixed dimension and $Q\|_W$ varies smoothly through positive definite operators.
  - Explanation: Establishes that integrating the degree-two homogeneous extension of an admissible map over the unit sphere yields a continuous, nonnegative, degree-one homogeneous operator map on the positive semidefinite cone whose coordinate sum equals the trace and whose positive support matches the union of line supports on the operator range.
- **C6** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-signed-extension)): The map $F:\operatorname{Sym}(V)\to\mathbb R^m$ is continuous, odd, and homogeneous of degree one for nonnegative scalars. It satisfies $$\\|F(A)\\|_1=\operatorname{tr}\|A\|, \qquad \sum_iF(A)_i=\operatorname{tr}A. \label{eq:extension-norms}$$ More precisely, its positive and negative label sets are $$\begin{aligned} \{i:F(A)_i>0\}&=\bigcup_{x\in\mathbb P(\operatorname{ran}A_+)}S(x),\\ \{i:F(A)_i<0\}&=\bigcup_{x\in\mathbb P(\operatorname{ran}A_-)}S(x). \end{aligned} \label{eq:spectral-support}$$ If every coordinate of $F(A)$ is nonzero, then $A$ is nonsingular. If all coordinates of $F(A)$ are strictly negative, then $A$ is negative definite; the analogous statement holds with both signs reversed. The map $F$ is smooth on the open set of nonsingular symmetric operators. In particular, it restricts to a continuous odd map $$\{A:\operatorname{tr}\|A\|=1\}\longrightarrow \{y\in\mathbb R^m:\\|y\\|_1=1\}. \label{eq:norm-sphere-map}$$
  - Explanation: Shows that subtracting the positive extension values on the positive and negative spectral parts of a symmetric operator produces a continuous odd map that preserves trace and trace norm, is smooth on nonsingular symmetric operators, and restricts to an odd map between the trace-norm sphere and the l1-norm sphere.
- **C7** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-label-bound)): If $k\ge2$, every smooth admissible map $f:\mathbb P(\mathbb R^k)\to\Delta^{m-1}$ satisfies $m\ge N_k$. If $m=N_k$, its extension $F$ has mod-two degree one and is surjective. In this equality case, every pair of labels is an edge of the support complex of $f$.
  - Explanation: Proves via the odd sphere extension that any smooth admissible map from projective space into a standard simplex requires at least as many labels as the dimension of the symmetric operator space, and that at the equality threshold the extension has mod-two degree one, is surjective, and forces every pair of labels to form an edge in the support complex.
- **C8** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-local-degree)): Let $M$ be a closed smooth $n$-manifold, possibly disconnected, and let $q:M\to Y$ be continuous, with $Y$ Hausdorff. Suppose a point $y\in Y$ has a neighborhood homeomorphic to an open subset of $\mathbb R^n$, and that $q$ is smooth in the corresponding coordinates near $q^{-1}(y)$, with invertible derivative at each point of that fiber. Then the fiber is finite, and the image of the total fundamental class of $M$ in $$H_n(Y,Y\setminus\{y\})\cong\mathbb F_2$$ is $\|q^{-1}(y)\|$ modulo two. In particular, if $Y$ is a connected closed $n$-manifold, this parity is the mod-two degree of $q$.
  - Explanation: Provides a local regular-value counting lemma over the two-element field showing that for a continuous map from a closed smooth manifold to a Hausdorff space that is smooth with invertible derivative near a fiber, the fiber is finite and its cardinality modulo two gives the mod-two degree without requiring global smoothness away from the fiber.
- **C9** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-equality-complex)): Let $k\ge3$ and $m=N_k$. Suppose $f:\mathbb P(\mathbb R^k)\to\Delta^{m-1}$ is a smooth admissible map. Its support complex $K$ has every pair of labels as an edge, every facet has $k$ vertices, and $$\sum_{I\text{ a facet of }K} I$$ is a simplicial cycle over $\mathbb F_2$. Consequently every $(k-2)$-dimensional face lies in a positive even number of facets.
  - Explanation: Proves that when the number of labels equals the symmetric matrix space dimension for dimension at least three, the support complex of a smooth admissible map has every label pair as an edge, is pure with every facet having size equal to the vector space dimension, and has the sum of all facets forming a simplicial cycle over the two-element field.
- **C10** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-six-from-map)): The triangles of the support complex of an admissible map $f:\mathbb{RP}^2\to\Delta^5$ form a six-label triangle system.
  - Explanation: Establishes that the triangular faces in the support complex of an admissible map from the real projective plane into the five-dimensional simplex form a six-label triangle system.
- **C11** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-six-properties)): Let $\mathcal T$ be a six-label triangle system on $C$.* 1. *Every vertex link is a cycle of length five.* 2. *Every four-element subset of $C$ contains a triangle, but does not contain all four of its triples as triangles.* 3. *If $a,b\in C$ are distinct and $R\subset C\setminus\{a,b\}$ has cardinality three, the link graphs at $a$ and $b$, restricted to $R$, are different. In particular, no transposition of two labels preserves $\mathcal T$.* 4. *The triangles contained in any five-element subset of $C$ determine $\mathcal T$ uniquely.
  - Explanation: Derives four structural rigidity properties of any six-label triangle system: every vertex link is a five-cycle, every four-label subset contains at least one and at most three triangles, restricted link graphs on any three remaining vertices differ for distinct vertices so no label transposition preserves the system, and the triangles on any five-element subset uniquely determine the whole system.
- **C12** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-facet-complement)): Suppose $f:\mathbb{RP}^3\to\Delta^9$ is admissible, let $K$ be its support complex, and let $S$ be a tetrahedron of $K$. The triangles of $K$ contained in the six-element set $C=[10]\setminus S$ form a six-label triangle system. In particular, two tetrahedra of $K$ cannot be disjoint.
  - Explanation: Proves that for any admissible map from real projective three-space into the nine-dimensional simplex, the triangles of its support complex contained in the six-label complement of any tetrahedral facet form a six-label triangle system, which implies that no two tetrahedra of the support complex can be disjoint.
- **C13** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-two-tetrahedra)): Every triangle of $K$ belongs to exactly two tetrahedra.
  - Explanation: Shows that in the ten-label support complex of an admissible map on real projective three-space, every triangular face belongs to exactly two tetrahedra because three tetrahedra sharing a triangle would induce a label transposition preserving the complementary six-label triangle system.
- **C14** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-short-links)): Every connected component of an edge link is a cycle of length three or four.
  - Explanation: Proves that every connected component of the link graph of an edge in the ten-label support complex is a simple cycle of length three or four, ruling out paths on five distinct vertices by transporting complementary six-label triangle systems.
- **C15** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-partner-blocks)): Fix a tetrahedron $S$ of $K$. For $v\in S$, let $p(v)$ be the unique label outside $S$ such that $(S\setminus\{v\})\cup\{p(v)\}$ is a tetrahedron. For each distinct value $y$ of $p$, set $$B_y=p^{-1}(y)\cup\{y\}.$$ The sets $B_y$ are disjoint and have at least two elements. For every choice $q_y\in B_y$, the set $$\bigcup_{y\in p(S)}(B_y\setminus\{q_y\})$$ is a tetrahedron. Moreover, every tetrahedron of $K$ contains at least two elements of one of these blocks.
  - Explanation: Organizes the vertices of a fixed tetrahedron and their unique exterior replacement partners across its four triangular faces into disjoint partner blocks of size at least two, proving that omitting one element from each block always forms a tetrahedron and every tetrahedron of the complex contains at least two elements of some block.
- **C16** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-no-ten-labels)): There is no admissible map $\mathbb{RP}^3\to\Delta^9$.
  - Explanation: Establishes that no admissible map exists from real projective three-space into the nine-dimensional standard simplex by showing that the partner-block decomposition forces block sizes three, two, and two with three remaining labels, which produces a triangle in the complement belonging to at most one tetrahedron.
- **C17** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-cor-higher-dimensions)): For every integer $d\geq9$, there is a compact subset of $\mathbb R^d$ of diameter $\sqrt2$ that cannot be covered by $d+1$ subsets of strictly smaller diameter.
  - Explanation: Extends the nine-dimensional counterexample to every higher Euclidean dimension by translating the rank-one projector set to the trace-zero subspace and adjoining orthogonal vectors with a positive definite Gram matrix so that all new points lie at diameter distance from the translated projector set and from one another.

        }

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-signed-extension",
          "exact_quote": "The map $F:\\operatorname{Sym}(V)\\to\\mathbb R^m$ is continuous, odd, and homogeneous of degree one for nonnegative scalars. It satisfies $$\\|F(A)\\|_1=\\operatorname{tr}|A|,\n \\qquad \\sum_iF(A)_i=\\operatorname{tr}A.\n \\label{eq:extension-norms}$$ More precisely, its positive and negative label sets are $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\{i:F(A)_i>0\\}&=\\bigcup_{x\\in\\mathbb P(\\operatorname{ran}A_+)}S(x),\\\\\n \\{i:F(A)_i<0\\}&=\\bigcup_{x\\in\\mathbb P(\\operatorname{ran}A_-)}S(x).\n\\end{aligned}\n \\label{eq:spectral-support}$$ If every coordinate of $F(A)$ is nonzero, then $A$ is nonsingular.           "source_id": "canonical:PM-156.1"
        },

          "exact_quote": "Any line in $\\operatorname{ran}A_+$ is orthogonal to every line in $\\operatorname{ran}A_-$.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-156.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-degree",
        "exact_quote": "Then the fiber is finite, and the image of the total fundamental class of $M$ in $$H_n(Y,Y\\setminus\\{y\\})\\cong\\mathbb F_2$$ is $|q^{-1}(y)|$ modulo two.",
        "source_id": "canonical:PM-156.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-degree",
          "exact_quote": "Let $M$ be a closed smooth $n$-manifold, possibly disconnected, and let $q:M\\to Y$ be continuous, with $Y$ Hausdorff. Suppose a point $y\\in Y$ has a neighborhood homeomorphic to an open subset of $\\mathbb R^n$, and that $q$ is smooth in the corresponding coordinates near $q^{-1}(y)$, with invertible derivative at each point of that fiber. Then the fiber is finite, and the image of the total fundamental class of $M$ in $$H_n(Y,Y\\setminus\\{y\\})\\cong\\mathbb F_2$$ is $|q^{-1}(y)|$ modulo two. In particular, if $Y$ is a connected closed $n$-manifold, this parity is the mod-two degree of $q$.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-156.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-local-degree",
          "exact_quote": "The inverse function theorem makes every point of the fiber isolated.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-156.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $M$ be a closed smooth $n$-manifold, possibly disconnected, and let $q:M\\to Y$ be continuous, with $Y$ Hausdorff. Then the fiber is finite, and the image of the total fundamental class of $M$ in $$H_n(Y,Y\\setminus\\{y\\})\\cong\\mathbb F_2$$ is $|q^{-1}(y)|$ modulo two. In particular, if $Y$ is a connected closed $n$-manifold, this parity is the mod-two degree of $q$.",
      "explanation": "Provides a local regular-value counting lemma over the two-element field showing that for a continuous map from a closed smooth manifold to a Hausdorff space that is smooth with invertible derivative near a fiber, the fiber is finite and its cardinality modulo two gives the mod-two degree without requiring global smoothness away from the fiber.",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-six-properties",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal T$ be a six-label triangle system on $C$.*\n\n1.  *Every vertex link is a cycle of length five.*\n\n2.  *Every four-element subset of $C$ contains a triangle, but does not contain all four of its triples as triangles.*\n\n3.  *If $a,b\\in C$ are distinct and $R\\subset C\\setminus\\{a,b\\}$ has cardinality three, the link graphs at $a$ and $b$, restricted to $R$, are different.           "source_id": "canonical:PM-156.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-six-properties",
          "exact_quote": "A transposition $(a\\ b)$ preserving the triangle system would make the two restricted link graphs identical, which is impossible.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-156.1"
        }

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **b(Y)** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a bounded set $Y$ of positive diameter, write $b(Y)$ for the least number of such subsets needed to cover $Y$.
- **Sym_4(R)** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Let $\operatorname{Sym}_4(\mathbb R)$ be the space of real symmetric $4\times4$ matrices, with Frobenius norm $\\|A\\|_F^2=\operatorname{tr}(A^2)$.
- **P_x** ([source](#sec:geometry)): For a line $x=[u]$, represented by a unit vector $u$, write $P_x=uu^{\mathsf T}$ for the orthogonal projector onto $x$.
- **N_k and X_k** ([source](#sec:geometry)): Let $\operatorname{Sym}(V)$ be the space of real symmetric operators, equipped with the Frobenius inner product $\langle A,B\rangle_F=\operatorname{tr}(AB)$, and put $$N_k=\dim\operatorname{Sym}(V)=\frac{k(k+1)}2, \qquad X_k=\{P_x:x\in\mathbb P(V)\}.$$
- **admissible map** ([source](#stmt-def-admissible)): A smooth map $f=(f_1,\ldots,f_m):\mathbb P(V)\to\mathbb R^m$ is *admissible* if each $f_i$ is nonnegative, $\sum_i f_i=1$, and, writing $S(x)=\{i:f_i(x)>0\}$, $$x\perp y\quad\Longrightarrow\quad S(x)\cap S(y)=\varnothing. \label{eq:admissibility}$$ Here smoothness means smoothness of the real coordinate functions on the usual smooth manifold $\mathbb P(V)$.
- **support complex K** ([source](#sec:geometry)): For an admissible map, define its *support complex* $K$ on the label set $\{1,\ldots,m\}$ by $$I\in K\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad I\subseteq S(x)\ \text{for some }x\in\mathbb P(V).$$
- **positive extension E_V** ([source](#eq:extension)): For a positive semidefinite operator $Q$, let $$E_V(Q)=k\int_{\mathbb S(V)}H(Q^{1/2}u)\,d\mu_V(u), \label{eq:extension}$$ where $\mu_V$ is rotation-invariant probability measure on the unit sphere.
- **signed extension F** ([source](#stmt-lem-signed-extension)): The map $F:\operatorname{Sym}(V)\to\mathbb R^m$ is continuous, odd, and homogeneous of degree one for nonnegative scalars. It satisfies $$\\|F(A)\\|_1=\operatorname{tr}\|A\|, \qquad \sum_iF(A)_i=\operatorname{tr}A. \label{eq:extension-norms}$$
- **norm spheres Sigma(V) and Sigma_m** ([source](#sec:topology)): For a Euclidean space $V$ of dimension $k$, write $$\Sigma(V)=\{A\in\operatorname{Sym}(V):\operatorname{tr}\|A\|=1\}, \qquad \Sigma_m=\{y\in\mathbb R^m:\textstyle\sum_i\|y_i\|=1\}.$$
- **six-label triangle system** ([source](#stmt-def-six-system)): A *six-label triangle system* on a set $C$ of cardinality six is a collection $\mathcal T\subseteq\binom{C}{3}$ such that 1. exactly one of $T$ and $C\setminus T$ belongs to $\mathcal T$ for each $T\in\binom{C}{3}$; 2. every pair of elements of $C$ belongs to exactly two members of $\mathcal T$.
- **partner blocks B_y** ([source](#stmt-lem-partner-blocks)): Fix a tetrahedron $S$ of $K$. For $v\in S$, let $p(v)$ be the unique label outside $S$ such that $(S\setminus\{v\})\cup\{p(v)\}$ is a tetrahedron. For each distinct value $y$ of $p$, set $$B_y=p^{-1}(y)\cup\{y\}.$$

          "exact_quote": "A *six-label triangle system* on a set $C$ of cardinality six is a collection $\\mathcal T\\subseteq\\binom{C}{3}$ such that\n\n1.  exactly one of $T$ and $C\\setminus T$ belongs to $\\mathcal T$ for each $T\\in\\binom{C}{3}$;\n\n2.  every pair of elements of $C$ belongs to exactly two members of $\\mathcal T$.",
          "source_id": "canonical:PM-156.1"
        }
      ],

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **S1** [claim](#stmt-lem-projector-geometry) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-projector-geometry): Embed real projective space homeomorphically into the trace-one affine hyperplane of symmetric operators via rank-one orthogonal projectors and compute pairwise Frobenius distances from inner products. -> Homeomorphic identification with the compact projector locus of diameter equal to the square root of 2 where diameter distance characterizes orthogonal lines.
- **S2** [claim](#stmt-lem-cover-to-map) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cover-to-map): Thicken each covering subset of strictly smaller diameter into a relatively open neighborhood of diameter strictly less than the square root of 2 and apply a subordinate smooth partition of unity. -> Smooth admissible map from projective space into the standard simplex whose positive supports are disjoint on orthogonal lines.
- **S3** [claim](#stmt-lem-positive-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-positive-extension): Extend the admissible map homogeneously of degree 2 to the vector space and integrate against rotation-invariant probability measure on the unit sphere after applying the positive matrix square root. -> Continuous degree-one homogeneous extension on the positive semidefinite cone preserving trace, subspace restrictions, and positive coordinate supports.
- **S4** [claim](#stmt-lem-signed-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-signed-extension): Decompose any symmetric operator into orthogonal positive and negative spectral parts and subtract their positive extensions to obtain a continuous odd map between trace-norm and l1-norm spheres. -> Continuous odd map on symmetric operators that is smooth on nonsingular operators and restricts to a map between unit norm spheres.
- **S5** [claim](#stmt-thm-label-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-label-bound): Apply the odd sphere map theorem to the norm-sphere restriction of the signed extension to lower-bound the label count and inspect preimages with two positive coordinates at equality. -> Lower bound on the number of labels and proof that at equality the extension has mod-two degree 1 and every pair of labels forms an edge of the support complex.
- **S6** [claim](#stmt-thm-equality-complex) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-equality-complex): Trivialize the orthogonal-complement sphere bundle over a regular level set in an affine projective chart, compare local mod-two preimage parity with first Stiefel-Whitney classes to force facet size k, and evaluate top relative homology. -> Purity of the support complex with k-vertex facets and proof that the sum of all facets is a mod-two simplicial cycle so every codimension-one face lies in a positive even number of facets.
- **S7** [claim](#stmt-lem-facet-complement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-facet-complement): Analyze the equality support complex on the projective plane to establish the properties of six-label triangle systems and restrict an admissible map on projective three-space to the orthogonal complement of a witness line for a tetrahedral facet. -> Proof that the triangles on the six-label complement of any tetrahedral facet form a six-label triangle system and that no two tetrahedra are disjoint.
- **S8** [claim](#stmt-thm-no-ten-labels) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-no-ten-labels): Transport complementary six-label triangle systems across adjacent tetrahedra to prove that every triangle lies in 2 tetrahedra, edge links have cycles of length 3 or 4, and partner blocks have sizes 3, 2, 2 with 3 exterior labels, yielding a triangle with at most 1 tetrahedral extension. -> Nonexistence of any admissible map from real projective three-space to the nine-dimensional simplex, proving the nine-dimensional and higher-dimensional Borsuk counterexamples.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **P3-I1**: The rank-one orthogonal projector map identifies real projective space over a k-dimensional Euclidean space homeomorphically with a compact subset of diameter equal to the square root of two inside the (N_k - 1)-dimensional trace-one affine hyperplane of symmetric operators, where two projectors attain diameter distance if and only if their lines are orthogonal, an explicit nine-coordinate linear map gives a Euclidean isometry for four-by-four trace-one symmetric matrices, and any cover by at most m subsets of diameter strictly less than the square root of two yields a smooth admissible map into the standard simplex.
- **P3-I2**: Averaging the degree-two homogeneous lift of a smooth admissible map over the unit sphere defines a continuous positive-homogeneous extension on the positive semidefinite cone whose coordinate sum equals the operator trace, and subtracting values on the positive and negative spectral parts yields a continuous odd map on all symmetric operators whose one-norm equals the trace norm and whose restriction between unit trace-norm and one-norm spheres is smooth on nonsingular operators.
- **P3-I3**: Every smooth admissible map from real projective (k - 1)-space into the (m - 1)-simplex requires at least N_k = k(k + 1)/2 labels, and at equality m = N_k for k at least three its support complex contains every pair of labels as an edge, has facets of uniform cardinality k, and forms a mod-two simplicial cycle so that every (k - 2)-dimensional face lies in a positive even number of facets.
- **P3-I4**: A six-label triangle system on a six-element set C selects ten triples containing one member of each complementary pair and covering every pair of labels twice; in every such system each vertex link is a five-cycle, every four-element subset contains between one and three triangles, any two distinct vertices have distinct link restrictions on every three-element subset of the remaining four vertices so no transposition preserves the system, and the triangles inside any five-element subset uniquely determine the entire system.
- **P3-I5**: In the support complex of any hypothetical admissible map from real projective three-space into the nine-simplex, every tetrahedral facet induces a six-label triangle system on its six-label complement, every triangle belongs to two tetrahedra, every edge-link component is a cycle of length three or four, and the partner blocks of any fixed tetrahedron force three blocks of sizes three, two, and two with three exterior labels, leaving a complementary triangle with at most one tetrahedral extension and ruling out any such admissible map.
- **P3-I6**: The compact set of rank-one orthogonal projectors on four-dimensional real space has Frobenius diameter equal to the square root of two and cannot be covered by ten subsets of strictly smaller diameter, and translating it to trace-zero symmetric matrices of squared norm 3/4 while adjoining s orthogonal vectors with Gram matrix I_s + (1/4) 1 1^T of squared norm 5/4 and mutual squared distance two yields compact subsets of diameter equal to the square root of two in every dimension d = 9 + s that cannot be covered by d + 1 subsets of strictly smaller diameter.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **P3-A1**: Apply the partition-of-unity reduction to an arbitrary ten-coloring or cover of the projector set that merely excludes individual orthogonal pairs without a uniform strict diameter gap below the square root of two. -> Without a strict inequality between the diameter of each covering set and the square root of two, thickening the sets by open metric neighborhoods can create orthogonal pairs inside the enlarged sets, destroying disjointness of positive coordinate supports.
- **P3-A2**: Treat the support complex of an admissible map as a triangulation of real projective three-space and invoke Walkup eleven-vertex lower bound for triangulations of real projective three-space to rule out ten labels directly. -> The faces of the support complex record subsets of positive coordinates of a smooth map into the simplex rather than simplices of a manifold triangulation, so manifold triangulation vertex bounds do not apply to the support complex without first proving face-incidence and cycle properties from the map.
- **P3-A3**: Cite Theorem 1 as establishing the exact Borsuk number b(X) of the four-dimensional rank-one projector set or as proving that dimension nine is the smallest dimension in which Borsuk conjecture fails. -> The theorem proves only the strict lower bound b(X) > 10 in dimension nine and neither computes an upper covering bound for X nor resolves whether counterexamples exist in dimensions below nine.
- **P3-A4**: Apply smooth differential degree and regular-value preimage arguments to the signed sphere map F at target vectors with zero coordinates or at singular symmetric operators. -> When a target coordinate vanishes, a preimage operator can have a nontrivial kernel where the positive and negative spectral projections and trace norm fail to be smooth, invalidating local Jacobian invertibility.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. Constructive finite verification of the combinatorial and algebraic steps in Theorem 1, Lemma 2, Remark 3, Definition 11, Lemma 13, Lemma 17, Theorem 18, and Corollary 19: (1) exact rational verification of the projector Frobenius distance formula, the explicit nine-coordinate Euclidean isometry on trace-one symmetric 4x4 matrices, the translated trace-zero projector squared norm 3/4, and the positive-definite Gram extension I_s + (1/4) 1 1^T with squared norm 5/4 and mutual squared distance 2; (2) exhaustive verification of six-label triangle systems and all four properties of Lemma 13 (5-cycle vertex links, 4-subset triangle counts between 1 and 3, distinct restricted links on 3-element subsets ruling out transposition symmetries, and unique reconstruction from 5-element subsets); and (3) finite verification of the ten-label partner-block obstruction in Lemma 17 and Theorem 18 showing that every valid block partition of 4 + t labels fails either the block-intersection bound, the exterior 4-subset triangle bound, or the two-tetrahedra extension condition of Lemma 15. The smooth partition-of-unity reduction (Lemma 5), spherical integration extension (Lemma 6), and topological Stiefel-Whitney cohomology steps (Theorem 10) involve continuous manifolds and are not certified by this finite code.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object with candidate triples triangles on {0..5}, block_sizes, 4-vectors u, v, and integer s.
# PRECONDITIONS: triangles has 3-lists in 0..5; block_sizes has ints >= 2 with sum(b - 1) == 4; u, v nonzero in Z^4; 1 <= s <= 6.
# OUTPUTS: JSON object with block_config_obstructed, cor19_gram_holds, frob_dist_sq, frob_formula_holds, gram_det, is_six_system, isometry_r9_holds, lemma13_holds, min_r_tet_extensions, r_avoids_four_subset, s_meets_block_twice.
# TIME: O(1) over subsets of {0..5} and {0..9} plus O(s^3) exact rational operations for the s x s Gram matrix.
# SPACE: O(1) for the 6-label and 10-label combinatorial structures plus O(s^2) for fmpq_mat storage.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact rational arithmetic via python-flint fmpq and fmpq_mat; no floating-point operations.
# FAILURE: Raises AssertionError, KeyError, or ValueError on malformed input or violated preconditions.
import itertools, json, sys
from flint import fmpq, fmpq_mat

data = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())
tri_in = data["triangles"]
bsizes = [int(x) for x in data["block_sizes"]]
u_vec = [int(x) for x in data["u"]]
v_vec = [int(x) for x in data["v"]]
s_dim = int(data["s"])

assert len(u_vec) == 4 and len(v_vec) == 4 and any(u_vec) and any(v_vec)
assert 1 <= s_dim <= 6
assert all(b >= 2 for b in bsizes) and sum(b - 1 for b in bsizes) == 4

c_all = tuple(range(6))
t_set = set()
for tr in tri_in:
    assert len(tr) == 3 and len(set(tr)) == 3 and all(0 <= int(x) < 6 for x in tr)
    t_set.add(tuple(sorted(int(x) for x in tr)))

all_triples = list(itertools.combinations(c_all, 3))
comp_ok = (len(t_set) == 10) and all(
    (tuple(sorted(set(c_all) - set(tr))) not in t_set) for tr in t_set
)
pair_ok = all(
    sum(1 for tr in t_set if p[0] in tr and p[1] in tr) == 2
    for p in itertools.combinations(c_all, 2)
)
is_six_system = bool(comp_ok and pair_ok)

lemma13_holds = False
if is_six_system:
    links_5cycle = True
    for a in c_all:
        rem = [x for x in c_all if x != a]
        edges = [tuple(sorted(set(tr) - {a})) for tr in t_set if a in tr]
        deg_ok = all(sum(1 for e in edges if v in e) == 2 for v in rem)
        adj = {v: [] for v in rem}
        for x, y in edges:
            adj[x].append(y)
            adj[y].append(x)
        seen = {rem[0]}
        stack = [rem[0]]
        while stack:
            cur = stack.pop()
            for nxt in adj[cur]:
                if nxt not in seen:
                    seen.add(nxt)
                    stack.append(nxt)
        if not (deg_ok and len(seen) == 5):
            links_5cycle = False
    four_ok = all(
        1 <= sum(1 for tr in itertools.combinations(sub4, 3) if tr in t_set) <= 3
        for sub4 in itertools.combinations(c_all, 4)
    )
    trans_ok = True
    for a, b in itertools.combinations(c_all, 2):
        others = [x for x in c_all if x not in (a, b)]
        for r3 in itertools.combinations(others, 3):
            la = {tuple(sorted(set(tr) - {a})) for tr in t_set if a in tr and set(tr) - {a} <= set(r3)}
            lb = {tuple(sorted(set(tr) - {b})) for tr in t_set if b in tr and set(tr) - {b} <= set(r3)}
            if la == lb:
                trans_ok = False
    rec5_ok = True
    for z in c_all:
        sub5_tris = {tr for tr in t_set if z not in tr}
        rebuilt = set(sub5_tris)
        for tr in all_triples:
            if z in tr:
                comp = tuple(sorted(set(c_all) - set(tr)))
                if comp not in sub5_tris:
                    rebuilt.add(tr)
        if rebuilt != t_set:
            rec5_ok = False
    lemma13_holds = bool(links_5cycle and four_ok and trans_ok and rec5_ok)

t_blocks = len(bsizes)
r_size = 6 - t_blocks
s_meets_block_twice = any(b - 1 >= 2 for b in bsizes)
r_avoids_four_subset = (r_size <= 3)

min_r_tet_extensions = -1
if is_six_system and sorted(bsizes) == [2, 2, 3]:
    blocks = []
    cur = 6
    for idx, b in enumerate(bsizes):
        omitted = idx
        present = list(range(cur, cur + b - 1))
        cur += b - 1
        blocks.append([omitted] + present)
    r_labels = {3, 4, 5}
    r_tris = [tr for tr in t_set if len(set(tr) & r_labels) >= 2]
    ext_counts = []
    for tr in r_tris:
        valid_exts = 0
        for x in range(10):
            if x not in tr:
                tet = set(tr) | {x}
                if any(len(tet & set(blk)) >= 2 for blk in blocks):
                    valid_exts += 1
        ext_counts.append(valid_exts)
    min_r_tet_extensions = min(ext_counts) if ext_counts else 0

block_config_obstructed = (
    (not s_meets_block_twice)
    or (not r_avoids_four_subset)
    or (is_six_system and sorted(bsizes) == [2, 2, 3] and 0 <= min_r_tet_extensions < 2)
)

u_norm_sq = sum(x * x for x in u_vec)
v_norm_sq = sum(x * x for x in v_vec)
uv_dot = sum(u_vec[i] * v_vec[i] for i in range(4))

pu = fmpq_mat(4, 4, [fmpq(u_vec[i] * u_vec[j], u_norm_sq) for i in range(4) for j in range(4)])
pv = fmpq_mat(4, 4, [fmpq(v_vec[i] * v_vec[j], v_norm_sq) for i in range(4) for j in range(4)])
diff_mat = pu - pv
diff_sq_mat = diff_mat * diff_mat
frob_dist_sq = sum((diff_sq_mat[i, i] for i in range(4)), fmpq(0))
formula_rhs = fmpq(2) - fmpq(2) * fmpq(uv_dot * uv_dot, u_norm_sq * v_norm_sq)
frob_formula_holds = bool(frob_dist_sq == formula_rhs)

d11, d22, d33, d44 = diff_mat[0, 0], diff_mat[1, 1], diff_mat[2, 2], diff_mat[3, 3]
r9_diag = (
    (d11 - d22) ** 2 * fmpq(1, 2)
    + (d11 + d22 - fmpq(2) * d33) ** 2 * fmpq(1, 6)
    + (d11 + d22 + d33 - fmpq(3) * d44) ** 2 * fmpq(1, 12)
)
r9_off = sum(
    (fmpq(2) * (diff_mat[i, j] ** 2) for i in range(4) for j in range(i + 1, 4)),
    fmpq(0),
)
isometry_r9_holds = bool(r9_diag + r9_off == frob_dist_sq)

shift_q = fmpq(1, 4)
id4 = fmpq_mat(4, 4, [fmpq(1 if i == j else 0) for i in range(4) for j in range(4)])
yu = pu - id4 * shift_q
yu_sq = yu * yu
yu_norm_sq = sum((yu_sq[i, i] for i in range(4)), fmpq(0))

gram_entries = [
    fmpq(5, 4) if i == j else fmpq(1, 4)
    for i in range(s_dim)
    for j in range(s_dim)
]
gram_mat = fmpq_mat(s_dim, s_dim, gram_entries)
gram_det = gram_mat.det()
mutual_ok = all(
    gram_mat[i, i] + gram_mat[j, j] - fmpq(2) * gram_mat[i, j] == fmpq(2)
    for i in range(s_dim)
    for j in range(i + 1, s_dim)
)
cross_ok = all(yu_norm_sq + gram_mat[i, i] == fmpq(2) for i in range(s_dim))
cor19_gram_holds = bool(
    yu_norm_sq == fmpq(3, 4)
    and gram_det == fmpq(s_dim + 4, 4)
    and gram_det > fmpq(0)
    and mutual_ok
    and cross_ok
)

out = {
    "block_config_obstructed": bool(block_config_obstructed),
    "cor19_gram_holds": bool(cor19_gram_holds),
    "frob_dist_sq": str(frob_dist_sq),
    "frob_formula_holds": bool(frob_formula_holds),
    "gram_det": str(gram_det),
    "is_six_system": bool(is_six_system),
    "isometry_r9_holds": bool(isometry_r9_holds),
    "lemma13_holds": bool(lemma13_holds),
    "min_r_tet_extensions": int(min_r_tet_extensions),
    "r_avoids_four_subset": bool(r_avoids_four_subset),
    "s_meets_block_twice": bool(s_meets_block_twice),
}
print(json.dumps(out, sort_keys=True))
```

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |

| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper is a pure mathematical proof establishing that the compact set $X$ of rank-one orthogonal projectors on $\mathbb R^4$ with the Frobenius metric has diameter $\sqrt2$ and cannot be covered by ten subsets of diameter strictly less than $\sqrt2$. Software architectures, library APIs, runtime benchmarks, and production system contracts are ABSENT. |

| other (metric_and_convex_geometry) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | Metric and convex geometry is the primary domain of the paper. Theorem 1 proves that the compact set $X$ of rank-one orthogonal projectors in the nine-dimensional trace-one affine hyperplane of $\operatorname{Sym}_4(\mathbb R)$ has Frobenius diameter $\sqrt2$ and cannot be covered by ten subsets of diameter strictly less than $\sqrt2$, disproving Borsuk's partition conjecture in dimension nine, and Corollary 19 extends this counterexample to every Euclidean dimension $d\geq9$. |
| other (algebraic_and_differential_topology) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | Algebraic and differential topology provides the central bridge from smooth admissible maps $f:\mathbb P(\mathbb R^k)\to\Delta^{m-1}$ to finite simplicial cycles over $\mathbb F_2$. Section 3 and Section 4 construct the continuous odd sphere map $F:\Sigma(V)\to\Sigma_m$, apply mod-two sphere degree and local regular-value counting (Lemma 9), and use the first Stiefel-Whitney class pullback on the projective bundle over a regular level set $L$ to prove Proposition 8 and Theorem 10. |
| other (combinatorics) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | Finite extremal and topological combinatorics governs Section 5 and Section 6, where the topological cycle condition is converted into a finite contradiction. Lemma 12 and Lemma 13 establish the structure of six-label triangle systems $\mathcal T\subseteq\binom{C}{3}$, and Lemma 14 through Theorem 18 transport complementary triangle systems across adjacent tetrahedra to bound edge-link cycles to lengths three or four, organize vertices into partner blocks $B_y$ of sizes $3,2,2$ with $\|R\|=3$, and refute any admissible map $\mathbb{RP}^3\to\Delta^9$. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L01** (counterexample_scope): Theorem 1 proves that the compact rank-one orthogonal projector set in dimension 9 has Frobenius diameter equal to the square root of 2 and requires more than 10 smaller-diameter subsets to cover, so Borsuk's assertion fails in dimension 9, but the theorem neither determines the minimum counterexample dimension nor determines the exact covering number of the projector set. Blocked use: ["Claiming that dimension 9 is the smallest dimension in which Borsuk's assertion fails, or asserting an exact value for the covering number beyond the lower bound strictly above 10."]
- **L02** (counterexample_scope): The 9-dimensional counterexample is the full compact continuum of rank-one orthogonal projectors representing real projective 3-space, rather than an explicit finite point configuration or finite spherical code. Blocked use: ['Using the theorem directly as an explicit finite point set or finite two-distance graph counterexample in dimension 9 without a separate compactness or discretization reduction.']
- **L03** (hypothesis): The reduction from a covering of the projector space to a smooth admissible map into the simplex requires a finite cover by subsets whose Frobenius diameters are each strictly less than the square root of 2; arbitrary partitions or colorings that merely exclude individual orthogonal pairs without a positive diameter gap do not yield open neighborhoods or a smooth partition of unity. Blocked use: ['Applying the smooth admissible-map obstruction directly to arbitrary colorings or partitions of projective space whose parts have diameter equal to the square root of 2.']
- **L04** (hypothesis): Admissibility requires smooth real coordinate functions on projective space summing to 1 whose positive supports are disjoint on orthogonal lines, relying on the Frobenius distance identity where maximum distance occurs precisely when the underlying lines are orthogonal. Blocked use: ['Transferring the covering bound to discontinuous coordinate maps or to matrix norms where the diameter distance does not coincide with line orthogonality.']
- **L05** (edge_case): The signed operator extension is continuous on all symmetric operators and smooth on the open subset of nonsingular symmetric operators, while the trace-norm and ell-1 norm spheres are not globally smooth hypersurfaces; local differential regular-value arguments require nonsingular preimages of full-support target vectors. Blocked use: ['Assuming global smoothness of the signed extension at singular symmetric matrices with nontrivial kernel, or treating the trace-norm and ell-1 spheres as globally smooth submanifolds without radial normalization.']
- **L06** (hypothesis): The pure facet cardinality and mod-2 simplicial cycle structure of the support complex hold at the equality threshold of k(k+1)/2 labels for dimension parameter at least 3; when the number of labels strictly exceeds k(k+1)/2, the projective bundle cohomology class need not vanish and does not force equidimensional facets. Blocked use: ['Assuming that the support complex of an admissible map is pure of facet size k or forms a mod-2 cycle when the label count strictly exceeds the symmetric-matrix dimension threshold.']
- **L07** (formal_scope): Faces of the support complex record subsets of coordinate labels that are simultaneously positive at some line in projective space, not geometric simplices of a triangulation of projective space; classical minimum-vertex bounds for triangulations of real projective 3-space do not apply directly to the support complex. Blocked use: ['Directly invoking triangulation vertex lower bounds on real projective 3-space to rule out 10-label support complexes, or treating the support complex as a triangulation of projective space.']
- **L08** (nonconstructive): The topological derivation of the support complex properties at equality uses Sard's theorem to select regular values in open simplices and negative orthants, combined with mod-2 degree and Stiefel-Whitney cohomology classes, rather than an explicit constructive preimage solver. Blocked use: ['Treating the regular-value existence step in the level-set and sphere-bundle degree argument as an explicit constructive algorithm for finding preimage lines of general smooth maps.']
- **L09** (approximation): Continuity of the positive semidefinite extension is proved qualitatively by uniform polynomial approximation of the matrix square root on a compact spectral interval and spherical integration; no quantitative polynomial degree bound or numerical quadrature convergence rate is stated. Blocked use: ['Using the qualitative polynomial-approximation continuity proof as a certified numerical quadrature or matrix square-root error bound.']
- **L10** (edge_case): Corollary 19 extends the counterexample to every integer dimension at least 9 by translating the projector set to the trace-zero hyperplane and adjoining orthogonal vectors with Gram matrix equal to the identity plus 1/4 times the all-ones matrix; this point-adjunction construction only increases the ambient dimension above 9. Blocked use: ['Using the orthogonal Gram-matrix point adjunction to construct counterexamples in dimensions below 9 or to preserve manifold connectivity when adjoining isolated points in dimensions above 9.']
- **L11** (unknown): Explicit cardinality bounds for a finite sub-configuration counterexample in dimension 9, algorithmic complexity bounds, and software implementation benchmarks are ABSENT from the paper. Blocked use: ['Claiming finite mesh cardinalities in dimension 9, algorithmic runtime bounds, or software performance guarantees from this paper.']

> ## Scope
>
> Borsuk's conjecture predicts that every bounded subset of $\mathbb R^d$ can be covered by $d+1$ sets of strictly smaller diameter. The formalized counterexample is the compact set of rank-one orthogonal projectors onto lines in $\mathbb R^4$, with the Frobenius metric. It lies in the nine-dimensional affine space of trace-one symmetric matrices, has diameter $\sqrt2$, and cannot be covered by ten arbitrary sets of smaller diameter.

Comparator source `BorsukNine.lean` (826 bytes, sha256 `c097288178f7b71023274b9cc3e5b600fad2e9b4c85bd635b7bf06c307fc631c`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace BorsukNine

abbrev Vector4 := EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin 4)
abbrev Matrix4 := EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin 4 × Fin 4)

def projector (u : Vector4) : Matrix4 :=
  WithLp.toLp 2 (fun ij => u ij.1 * u ij.2)

def projectorSet : Set Matrix4 :=
  projector '' {u : Vector4 | ‖u‖ = 1}

def traceOneSymmetric : Set Matrix4 :=
  {A | (∀ i j : Fin 4, A (i, j) = A (j, i)) ∧ ∑ i : Fin 4, A (i, i) = 1}

def HasTenSmallCover : Prop :=
  ∃ C : Fin 10 → Set Matrix4,
    (∀ i, C i ⊆ projectorSet) ∧
    projectorSet ⊆ ⋃ i, C i ∧
    ∀ i, Metric.diam (C i) < Real.sqrt 2

theorem main_theorem :
    IsCompact projectorSet ∧
    projectorSet ⊆ traceOneSymmetric ∧
    Metric.diam projectorSet = Real.sqrt 2 ∧
    ¬ HasTenSmallCover := by
  sorry

end BorsukNine

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


The compact set of rank-one orthogonal projectors on ℝ<sup>4</sup>, with the Frobenius metric, cannot be covered by ten sets of strictly smaller diameter. It therefore gives a counterexample to Borsuk's conjecture in dimension nine.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
